C'est une liste dédiée, volontairement indépendante des rôles : un « rôle interdit de ticket » se contourne en le perdant, alors qu'ici l'exclusion porte sur le membre lui-même — elle survit à un changement de rôle, à un changement de pseudo, et à un départ suivi d'un retour. +blacklist add @membre [raison], remove, list (paginée) et check @membre. Le membre exclu voit pourquoi il est refusé : sans ça, il ouvre un ticket pour demander la raison… ce qu'il ne peut justement plus faire. Trois précisions : elle ne bannit personne et ne retire aucun rôle (c'est +bl, réservée aux owners, qui bannit à l'arrivée), le propriétaire du serveur ne peut pas y être ajouté, et un membre blacklisté après s'être inscrit à un giveaway est écarté du tirage — mais peut toujours se désinscrire lui-même.
  • 🧾 Le transcript d'un ticket contient enfin les réponses au formulaire — elles sont publiées dans un encadré attaché au message d'accueil ; l'archive ne gardait que le texte brut de ce message et écrivait [embed] à leur place. Autrement dit, le seul endroit où les réponses étaient consignées disparaissait avec le salon. Les encadrés sont désormais transcrits en plus du texte, jamais à la place.
  • 📟 L'astreinte est active par défaut — +oncall prévient l'équipe quand une alerte critique tombe pendant que personne n'est en ligne. Elle attendait d'être activée serveur par serveur, ce qui revenait à ne prévenir personne là où on ne savait pas qu'elle existait. Elle démarre maintenant activée partout, y compris sur les serveurs à venir. Les serveurs qui l'ont explicitement coupée le restent : la coupure est un choix, pas une absence de choix.
  • 💜 +soutien — récompense ceux qui affichent le tag de ton serveur — Discord permet à un membre de porter le tag d'un serveur à côté de son pseudo. Le bot le voit et attribue tout seul le ou les rôles de soutien (plusieurs rôles possibles), puis les retire dès que le tag n'est plus affiché. C'est du soutien vérifiable : c'est Discord qui tranche, pas une phrase à recopier dans son statut — et aucune permission supplémentaire n'est demandée au bot. La pose se fait à l'instant où le tag change, et un balayage de rattrapage repasse derrière pour les cas qu'un évènement ne peut pas couvrir (tag changé pendant que le bot était éteint). Sécurité identique au reste : jamais un rôle à permission sensible, jamais un rôle que le bot ne peut pas gérer, jamais aux bots — et un rôle donné à la main n'est jamais repris, seul ce que le bot a posé lui appartient.
  • 🔊 Un salon vocal réservé aux soutiens — depuis le menu +soutien, un soutien crée son salon vocal en un clic : visible de tout le monde, mais seuls les soutiens peuvent y entrer. Il vient avec le panneau de contrôle habituel (verrouiller, limiter les places, renommer, expulser) et disparaît tout seul dès qu'il se vide. Créé mais jamais rejoint, il est supprimé au bout de 5 minutes — sinon il resterait là pour toujours.
  • 🚦 Chaque type d'action peut avoir SON salon de logs — +setlogs routes ouvre un aiguillage : les bans dans #ban-logs, les expulsions ailleurs, les purges de messages encore ailleurs. Neuf actions précises (ban, déban, expulsion, mute, arrivées, rôles, rôles automatiques, purges, pseudos) et les dix catégories larges sont routables. Une action non réglée retombe sur sa catégorie, et une catégorie non réglée sur le salon général : ne rien configurer ne change donc rien au comportement actuel. Le bot vérifie qu'il peut réellement écrire dans le salon choisi — router vers un salon où il est muet ferait perdre les logs en silence. Et l'alerte « quelqu'un efface les logs » couvre désormais tous les salons routés, pas seulement le général.
  • 🧾 Les derniers messages d'un membre banni, gardés avec le ban — bannir quelqu'un fait souvent disparaître ce qu'il avait écrit (Discord propose d'effacer ses 7 derniers jours), et le contexte de la décision se perd exactement au moment où on en aura besoin : un appel de sanction, trois semaines plus tard. Le journal joint désormais ses derniers messages au log de bannissement, capturés avant que quoi que ce soit ne soit supprimé. Ce que la sécurité avait bloqué (phishing, mot de panique) n'est jamais ré-affiché — seule la raison l'est. Le log dit franchement qu'il montre ce qui restait en mémoire, pas l'historique complet. Désactivable.
  • 🤖 Les rôles automatiques ne noient plus les logs de rôles — rôle de soutien, grade de niveau, podium des jeux, rôle d'arrivée : le bot les pose et les retire des centaines de fois par jour, et ils enterraient le seul changement qu'on cherchait — celui fait à la main. Trois choix dans +setlogs routes : les laisser mélangés (comme avant), ne plus les journaliser du tout, ou leur donner leur propre récapitulatif, routable vers son propre salon. La liste des rôles concernés n'est écrite nulle part : elle est déduite de ce que chaque module a déjà réglé, donc un rôle de soutien ajouté est reconnu immédiatement.
  • ⏳ Fermer un salon « pour deux heures », et qu'il se rouvre tout seul — huit nouvelles commandes : +autolock, +autounlock, +autohide, +autounhide, et la variante « tous les salons » de chacune. +autolock #annonces 2h verrouille maintenant et déverrouille à l'heure dite, sans que personne ait à y penser. L'échéance est écrite en base, pas tenue par un minuteur en mémoire : un salon fermé le vendredi soir ne reste pas fermé tout le week-end parce que le bot a redémarré. Et surtout, à l'expiration le bot remet l'état d'avant — un salon qui était déjà fermé le reste, au lieu d'être ouvert par erreur.
  • 🎯 +permsalon — un rôle peut utiliser une commande dans UN salon précis — « l'animateur peut faire +clear dans #giveaway, mais pas dans #général ». L'autorisation est purement additive : elle ajoute un droit, elle n'en retire jamais aucun, à personne. Une autorisation posée sur une catégorie vaut pour tous ses salons. La liste des commandes délégables est fermée et vit dans le code — aucun réglage ne peut l'élargir : sont exclues tout ce qui touche à la sécurité ou à la configuration, tout ce qui porte plus loin que le salon où la commande est tapée (+purge … all efface partout), et les sanctions qui visent une personne (un bannissement ne se produit pas « dans un salon »).
  • 🙈 Ignorer certains salons dans la surveillance des salons — +antiupdate ignore #compteur-membres : les compteurs qui se renomment toutes les dix minutes et les vocaux temporaires ne remplissent plus le journal. Ignorer une catégorie couvre tous ses salons, y compris ceux créés plus tard. Deux garde-fous : une suppression en rafale (3 salons en 1 minute) est journalisée quoi qu'il arrive — c'est la signature d'un nuke, et la liste d'exclusion ne doit pas devenir l'endroit où l'on se cache ; et les protections anti-raid n'ont aucune connaissance de cette liste, elles continuent de compter et de sanctionner chaque suppression.
  • 🧵 Un fil s'ouvre tout seul sous chaque photo — +autothread #photos : chaque image postée reçoit son fil, pour que les réactions et les avis ne noient plus le salon. Un message sans image n'en reçoit pas — c'est le but. Le titre du fil est réglable ({user}, {sujet}), et le mode tous ouvre un fil sous chaque message si on préfère.
  • 🖼️ Salon « images uniquement » — +autothread onlypic on #photos : les messages sans image y sont supprimés, avec un rappel qui s'efface tout seul pour ne pas remplir le salon à son tour. Les membres qui peuvent gérer les messages en sont dispensés — sans cette exception, impossible d'écrire la consigne du salon.
  • 🐢 Un mode lent dans les fils créés automatiquement — +autothread lent 30 #photos : les fils ouverts par le bot naissent déjà en mode lent. Posé à la création et non après : entre les deux, il y a une fenêtre pendant laquelle le fil est ouvert sans son mode lent, et c'est exactement à cet instant que part une rafale.
  • 🎨 {MemberBanner} — la bannière du membre dans les messages personnalisés — utilisable dans le message de bienvenue, celui d'au revoir et les commandes personnalisées, aux côtés de deux nouvelles voisines : {MemberPic} (avatar) et {ServerBoostsCount} (nombre de boosts). La bannière n'est pas fournie par Discord avec le reste : elle demande une requête à part, qui n'est faite que si le message la réclame vraiment. Un membre sans bannière donne un texte vide, jamais « undefined ». Et la liste des variables affichée par +setwelcome, +setgoodbye et +custom est maintenant déduite de la vraie table — elle ne peut plus annoncer une variable qui n'existe pas, ni oublier celle qu'on vient d'ajouter.
  • 📦 Les caisses surprises disent enfin leur rareté — trois raretés : 🎁 Normale (87 %, 30-79 XP), ✨ Rare (10 %, 120-199 XP) et 💎 Légendaire (3 %, 300-499 XP + un peu d'aura). Elles existaient déjà, mais la rareté n'était nommée qu'à l'apparition : à la récupération, on savait ce qu'on gagnait sans savoir ce qu'on venait d'ouvrir. Elle est désormais annoncée au moment de la prise (emoji, couleur et nom), y compris dans le message privé de celui qui résout l'énigme d'une caisse verrouillée. La caisse dorée s'appelle maintenant Rare ; les classements de records ne bougent pas.
  • 📋 Les caisses récupérées passent enfin par le journal — leur trace court-circuitait le système de logs : elle écrivait directement dans le salon général. Elle ignorait donc l'aiguillage par action (+setlogs routes), n'alimentait ni le résumé quotidien ni le bilan de journée, et ne bénéficiait d'aucun réessai en cas de micro-coupure. C'est corrigé, avec la rareté, le gain et le mode d'ouverture (clic ou énigme) dans l'embed. Les caisses ont leur propre clé d'aiguillage : elles peuvent partir dans un salon à elles, et décocher une catégorie de surveillance ne les éteint pas.
  • 📌 Les épinglages deviennent lisibles — Discord annonce « X a épinglé un message » sans dire lequel et sans mener nulle part. +pinlog on remplace cet avis par un embed : qui a épinglé, un extrait du message, son auteur et un lien direct. Les épingles sont comparées à celles déjà connues, si bien qu'un épinglage fait pendant que le bot était éteint est retrouvé au changement suivant — et au redémarrage. Le tout premier passage sur un salon enregistre l'existant sans rien publier : activer le module sur un salon qui compte quarante épingles n'en publie pas quarante. Destination réglable.
  • 🔒 +lockrole — figer un rôle entièrement — un rôle verrouillé ne peut plus être modifié : ni son nom, ni ses permissions, ni sa couleur, ni son affichage séparé, ni sa mentionnabilité. Toute modification est annulée dans la seconde et journalisée avec son auteur. C'est la parade aux manipulations discrètes : renommer « Modérateur » en « Membre », ou lui retirer une permission sans que rien ne l'annonce. +unlockrole lève le verrou. Un rôle situé au-dessus du bot est refusé plutôt que d'accepter un verrou décoratif.
  • ↩️ Couper la vérification fait désormais le ménage — et reste réversible — +setverify off n'effaçait que le rôle : le salon restait, avec un bouton « Se vérifier » qui ne donnait plus rien, sans qu'un arrivant puisse le deviner. Le salon devenu inutile est maintenant supprimé, et les owners reçoivent une alerte avec un bouton « C'était une erreur — tout remettre » qui recrée le salon dans sa catégorie d'origine, rétablit le rôle et repose le panneau. Les trois façons de désactiver (commande, +setup, assistant) suivent enfin le même chemin.
  • 🌐 +translate — traduire un texte, ou le message auquel on répond — +translate en Bonjour tout le monde, ou bien réponds à un message avec +translate es. Quinze langues, écrites comme on les écrit vraiment (anglais, en, english). La langue d'origine est détectée toute seule. Le service gratuit utilisé plafonne à 500 caractères par traduction : c'est dit clairement quand on dépasse, plutôt que de découper le texte en morceaux qui se contrediraient. Réponse en cache pendant dix minutes, une traduction toutes les 3 secondes par serveur, et jamais de blocage : au-delà de 7 secondes sans réponse, le bot le dit et rend la main.
  • 👁️ +lastseen @membre — la dernière fois qu'un membre a été vu ACTIF — dernier message, dernière minute de vocal comptabilisée, ou « en vocal en ce moment » si c'est le cas. Ce n'est pas une dernière connexion : Discord ne la communique à personne, et la deviner exigerait de recevoir en permanence le statut de chaque membre de chaque serveur. Le bot ne le demande pas. La commande affiche donc d'après quoi elle répond, et le dit noir sur blanc — « vu il y a trois jours » ne veut pas dire « pas connecté depuis trois jours ».
  • ⏰ Les rappels comprennent enfin les heures — et le changement d'heure — en plus des délais (+remind 1h …), on peut écrire +remind 9h30 …, +remind demain 9h … ou +remind 25/12 18:00 …. Ces heures sont calculées avec le décalage de la date visée, pas celui du jour où on tape la commande : « le 15 novembre à 9 h » programmé en plein été tombe bien à 9 h, et non à 10 h. La nuit du passage à l'heure d'été, une heure demandée qui n'existe pas (la pendule saute de 2 h à 3 h) est signalée et repoussée au premier instant valable, au lieu d'être déplacée en douce. 9h tout seul reste un délai de 9 heures, comme avant.
  • 🗂️ Les suggestions peuvent devenir des posts de FORUM — indique un salon forum avec +setsuggest #idees et chaque +suggest y crée un post à part entière : on en débat dans son propre fil sans polluer les autres, on vote 👍 / 👎 comme avant, et quand le staff tranche, le post reçoit son étiquette (✅ Approuvée / ⛔ Refusée) puis se ferme. Le mode se déduit du type du salon — pas de réglage supplémentaire qui pourrait un jour contredire le salon réellement configuré. Les étiquettes déjà présentes sur ton forum (« bug », « idée »…) sont conservées telles quelles, identifiants compris : aucun post déjà classé ne perd son classement.
  • 💜 Le salon vocal de soutien porte enfin les couleurs du bot — il s'appelait « 💜 pseudo », ce qui ne disait pas ce qu'il était et le rendait indistinguable d'un vocal temporaire ordinaire. Il devient « 💜 Soutien · pseudo », dans la même signature que les autres salons créés par le bot (🏆 Salle des trophées, 🚩 signalements) : on lit d'abord à quoi sert le salon, puis à qui il appartient — l'ordre utile quand on parcourt la liste vocale.
  • ⚖️ +punish setup montre enfin la chaîne entière des strikes — d'un côté les paliers de sanction, de l'autre des seuils perdus dans « Réglages avancés », et rien qui disait que les seconds alimentent les premiers : régler « 5 strikes → ban » sans savoir ce qui compte un strike, c'est régler une moitié de mécanisme. Le panneau affiche maintenant ce qui génère un strike (scam, spam, mentions en masse, liens, mots interdits — avec l'état réel de chacun) puis ce que les strikes déclenchent. Et il prévient quand aucun module n'alimente les strikes alors que des paliers sont réglés : pris séparément, les deux écrans avaient l'air corrects — c'est leur mise côte à côte qui révèle le trou. Les seuils se règlent depuis les deux endroits, avec un seul enregistrement : les boutons du panneau appellent littéralement le code de +setup, donc les deux vues ne peuvent pas diverger. +setup renvoie à +punish en retour. (L'ancien +punish setup, qui appliquait l'échelle stricte d'un coup, reste accessible via +punish setup strict et par un bouton du panneau.)
  • 🔔 Les préférences d'alertes entrent dans l'assistant — +wizard a désormais une étape « Préférences d'alertes » dans le groupe Sécurité : comment le bot te signale une alerte dans le salon de logs (@everyone, @here, ou le rôle d'alerte, retenu par défaut). Le bouton ouvre le panneau de +alertconfig lui-même — pas une copie —, avec « Mes réglages personnels » qui restent privés.
  • 🔧 +update met désormais à jour le CODE, plus les paquets — la commande ne touchait en réalité pas au code : elle lançait npm install <paquet>@latest sur toutes les dépendances, puis redémarrait. Et @latest ignore la plage déclarée : un ^12.11.1 n'a pas empêché l'installation d'une version majeure suivante, avec réécriture de package.json au passage. Par défaut, +update fait maintenant un git pull du code, lance les tests, puis redémarre — sans toucher à un seul paquet. Les paquets restent accessibles par un mot explicite, +update deps, qui annonce les changements de version majeure avant d'installer. Trois garde-fous sur le pull : refus si des modifications ne sont pas commitées (ce dossier est la copie de travail du bot en production), avance rapide uniquement (jamais de fusion créée dans le dos), et aucun rembobinage automatique — le bot continue de tourner sur le code déjà chargé, il n'y a aucune urgence.
  • 💾 +backup ouvre un menu — plus besoin de retenir la syntaxe — le système de sauvegarde était complet mais entièrement en ligne de commande : cinq sous-commandes, trois périmètres, un mode de restauration et deux confirmations. +backup tout court affiche maintenant l'état des trois périmètres (complète, rôles + salons, messages) avec leurs décomptes réels et leur historique, et trois boutons : Créer (choix du périmètre), Restaurer (choix de la version) et Historique. Les sous-commandes restent là et ne changent pas : +backup roles, +backup list, +backup messages, +backup restore confirm font exactement ce qu'elles faisaient. Le menu est réservé aux owners du serveur, et la restauration reste réservée aux owners du bot — le contrôle est refait à chaque clic, car un panneau reste cliquable longtemps après avoir été ouvert.
  • ♻️ La restauration au clic propose enfin son mode — après avoir choisi le sort des salons du bot (garder / supprimer / recréer), on choisit désormais Intelligente, Simple ou Purge. Ce choix existait en ligne de commande mais le parcours à boutons appliquait le défaut en silence : impossible de corriger un salon dérivé ou de faire le ménage sans passer par le clavier. Le menu +backup et la proposition automatique d'après-attaque empruntent le même parcours — mêmes questions, mêmes contrôles, même application. Un bouton d'une version antérieure du bot continue de fonctionner à l'identique.
  • ⏳ Le bot rattrape les commandes tapées pendant qu'il était hors ligne — une coupure, quelqu'un tape +bot, et le message reste là sans réponse : personne ne sait s'il a été vu. Au retour, le bot relit les salons et répond au message d'origine (vraie réponse Discord) avec un mot qui explique le décalage. Ton message n'est jamais supprimé. Actif partout par défaut, réglable avec +rattrapage.

    La fenêtre n'a pas de limite de temps : une question posée il y a trois jours mérite encore sa réponse. C'est le volume qui borne — au plus 20 commandes par serveur et par redémarrage, les plus récentes d'abord, et ce qui dépasse est annoncé plutôt que tu. Un simple redémarrage (moins de 4 min d'arrêt) ne déclenche rien : sans ce plancher, chaque déploiement rejouerait des commandes.

    Seules des commandes de lecture sont rejouées (+bot, +level, +sanctions, +lastseen…), depuis une liste fermée, écrite dans le code, qu'aucun réglage ne peut élargir. Jamais de destructrices, jamais de sécurité ni de configuration, jamais de jeux ni d'économie — sur ces dernières, la personne a souvent retapé la commande au retour, et rejouer distribuerait la récompense deux fois. Les permissions sont re-vérifiées au moment du rattrapage, pas au moment où la commande avait été tapée : qui est parti, a été rétrogradé ou sanctionné entre-temps n'obtient rien. Et un serveur encore sous attaque est sauté.

    🖱️ Les clics de bouton ne sont pas rattrapables, et ce n'est pas une question d'effort : Discord ne livre une interaction que par la passerelle, ne la met pas en file et n'expose aucun moyen de retrouver celles qu'on a manquées. Les panneaux du bot restent valides — reclique, ça marche.
  • 📺 +createur — annonce les nouvelles vidéos YouTube d'une chaîne — suis jusqu'à 5 chaînes par serveur, choisis le salon d'annonce et la mention (un rôle, @everyone, ou aucune). Ajoute une chaîne par son lien, son @pseudo ou son identifiant : le bot retrouve le reste tout seul. À l'ajout, ses vidéos déjà en ligne sont mémorisées sans être annoncées — sinon le premier réglage posterait quinze vidéos d'un coup. Une vidéo n'est jamais annoncée deux fois, même si le flux se réorganise. Vérification toutes les 10 minutes, en rotation sur l'ensemble des serveurs, jamais plus de 8 chaînes relues par tour.

    Twitch et TikTok ne sont pas inclus, et ce n'est pas un oubli. L'API Twitch exige un identifiant + secret d'application (elle répond 401 sans) : à décider avant de l'ajouter. TikTok n'offre aucun moyen fiable et gratuit de détecter une publication — pas de flux, une page sans données, un oEmbed en erreur. Plutôt que d'improviser sur un relais tiers non officiel, c'est écrit noir sur blanc.
  • ✨ L'assistant de configuration devient lisible — seize étapes réparties en quatre thèmes, et jusqu'ici une rangée de seize carrés identiques pour toute boussole. La progression est maintenant une jauge avec pourcentage (▰▰▰▰▰▱▱▱▱▱ 50 % · étape 8/16 · 🏘️ Communauté 1/4) : on sait où on en est, dans le parcours et dans le thème. Le titre s'allège d'autant — il ne porte plus que le thème.
  • 🏠 Un sommaire pour l'assistant — nouveau bouton sur chaque étape : il affiche les seize étapes groupées par thème, cochées ou non, et permet de sauter directement à celle qu'on veut au lieu de cliquer « Retour » huit fois. Le bouton ⚙️ « Réglages détaillés » y a déménagé : la rangée d'une étape est désormais de la navigation pure (reculer · avancer · passer · sommaire · fermer), et ⚙️ ouvre de toute façon un panneau entièrement différent.
  • 🏁 Le récapitulatif final dit enfin ce qui a été fait — il annonçait « 11/16 étapes en place », ce qui ne dit pas lesquelles. Il liste maintenant nommément ce qui est en place et ce qui ne l'est pas encore, en rappelant que rien n'est obligatoire. Côté technique, l'état de chaque étape n'est plus calculé deux fois pour le même écran, et les positions dans les thèmes sont préparées une seule fois au démarrage plutôt qu'à chaque affichage.
  • 📖 Le +help parle enfin français — les 233 explications de commandes ont été relues une par une, et 29 réécrites. Les trop courtes disaient quoi sans dire comment : « Supprime des messages en masse » devient « Supprime d'un coup les derniers messages du salon (+clear 50) — Discord interdit d'effacer au-delà de 14 jours ». Le jargon a sauté : plus de « vanity URL » ni de « webhook » lâchés sans explication. Et les descriptions à rallonge, illisibles dans une liste, ont été resserrées sans rien perdre — celle de +setup passait de 412 à 203 caractères. Aucun nom, alias, catégorie ni comportement n'a bougé : seuls les textes changent.
  • 👑 +createur passe en main du propriétaire — la commande des notifications de créateurs était accessible à tout administrateur : n'importe lequel pouvait faire annoncer une chaîne dans le salon de son choix, avec la mention de son choix — @everyone compris, à chaque publication. C'est une décision de vitrine du serveur, pas un réglage de modération : elle est désormais réservée au propriétaire du serveur et aux owners du bot.
  • 🎭 Nouveau jeu — Fais-moi rire (+memegame) : la même image pour tout le monde, chacun écrit sa légende en secret, puis le salon vote pour la plus drôle sans savoir qui a écrit quoi. 3 à 10 joueurs, plusieurs manches sur des images différentes, révélation des auteurs à chaque fin de manche, puis classement final avec XP et aura. +memegame historique garde le palmarès du serveur — les 20 dernières parties et leur légende gagnante. L'anonymat est réel : l'ordre des propositions est mélangé et le bulletin de vote ne contient que des numéros, jamais l'identité des auteurs.
  • 🔤 +prefix reset — retour au préfixe par défaut en une commande. Utile quand un préfixe malheureux a été posé : +prefix off mettait littéralement off en préfixe, et il fallait deviner comment revenir en arrière.
  • 🇬🇧 Le bot commence à parler anglais — le drapeau 🇫🇷/🇬🇧 de l'assistant et de +setup n'était que décoratif : la langue choisie était bien enregistrée en base, mais aucun message ne la lisait. Elle est désormais effective. Les premiers écrans sont traduits — refus de permission, ossature de +help, écran de langue — et le reste suivra par étapes. Une traduction anglaise manquante n'affiche jamais d'erreur ni de texte technique : le message repasse simplement en français.
  • 🎖️ Choose how fast levels come — the XP curve is finally adjustable without touching anything technical: five settings, from Very fast to Very slow, with the real cost of levels 1, 5 and 10 shown before you choose. Available in +setup → Levels & XP, and straight from the step-by-step assistant. Levels already earned are never recalculated: only the rest of the progression changes.
  • 🔗 +vanitylist — who arrived through the custom URL — the new command lists members who joined via the server's vanity link (discord.gg/yourserver), with pagination. Discord does not report this: it is inferred by comparing invite counters on every arrival. The command says so plainly — a join is only attributed when no classic invite moves and the vanity counter goes up by exactly one. Simultaneous arrivals are therefore not counted, never guessed.
  • 🎉 The weekly gala wasn't getting lost on the way — it was lost every time — the edition was marked "done" before it was sent. Deleted channel, missing permission, bot restarted at the wrong moment: the evening was consumed with nobody seeing anything, and you had to wait another week. The marker is now written only after a successful delivery, and skipped servers are logged with the reason.
  • 👋 Welcomes tell a first visit from a return — welcome and goodbye messages now know whether someone is discovering the server or coming back, with new variables: {firstjoin}, {joincount}, {membercount}, {accountcreated} and {memberduration} (time spent on the server). A separate return message can be set, and the panel shows "✨ First visit" or "🔁 Back · how many times".
  • 🤖 +antibot on now enables smart mode — strict mode was the default, and it blocked legitimate bots added by the team. on and smart now do the same thing; strict mode remains available with +antibot strict.
  • 🔊 +voicemove finally accepts every natural way of writing it — move one person, a whole channel, several people, or one channel into another: combinations are recognised in the order you write them, mentions or IDs alike. Group moves are paced so Discord doesn't rate-limit them.
  • 🧭 The step-by-step assistant covers what it was missing — the starboard and the automatic join role can be enabled from the Community step, detailed log settings open with a button, and stat counters are at last all offered (including 👥 administrators and 🛡️ staff, bots excluded) with distinct emojis and a sensible order.
  • 🎨 Two theme colours were missing — yellow and cyan existed in +theme but not in the assistant, which in turn offered a black nothing else knew about. All three screens now read the same palette.
  • 🧱 The hardening summary no longer repeated itself three times — the security recap could ask for "entry verification" up to three times in the same message. The advice now appears once, and only if it isn't already listed above.
  • ♻️ +renew does the same as +nuke — same command, a clearer name for what it actually does: recreate a channel identically, emptied of its history.
  • 🎭 A role-menu button can now grant several roles at once — packs have arrived: one entry can carry up to 10 roles, one click adds every missing one, a second click removes them all. Every role still goes through the same checks as before (never a role with sensitive permissions, never one above the bot), verified role by role on every click. A given role can only belong to one entry of a menu, otherwise two buttons would undo each other.
  • 🎨 Every role-menu entry now has its own configuration screen — +rolemenu → Configure an entry: the roles granted, the label, the description, the emoji, the button colour (blue / grey / green / red, picked from a list instead of typed by hand) and above all the position in the menu, which could not be changed at all until now. Already-published menus keep working without being reposted.
  • 🔄 An update is no longer refused because a server is "on alert" — the block was absolute: a single server in MAX posture made +update unusable everywhere, indefinitely. It is now just a warning shown before the restart.
  • 🛡️ MAX security posture no longer stays on forever — set by hand (or inherited from an incident whose timer was lost on restart), it had no deadline and never came back down. It now returns to normal mode on its own after 2 h, with a message in the logs. The +panic incident mode keeps its "restore everything" button.
  • 📋 The log finally covers reactions, emojis and screen sharing — newly logged events, each filed under an existing category (so still switchable in +setup → Log types): reactions added and removed (Messages), emojis and stickers added / deleted / renamed (Server), threads renamed, archived or locked (Threads), screen share (Go Live) and camera started/stopped in voice (Voice). Reactions get their own grouped message: a burst of emoji no longer drowns — nor overwrites — message deletions. The bot never logs its own reactions or its own actions.
  • 🧾 Deleting a log no longer goes unnoticed — if someone deletes a message from the log channel, the bot reports it, with who did it and which log disappeared. Three safeguards prevent any loop: the report itself is never re-reported, it is recognised by its title even after a restart, and the bot's own housekeeping (removing its stale buttons) stays silent — only a deletion attributed to someone else raises the alert.
  • 🔢 The counting warning no longer arrives by DM — when someone counts twice in a row, the explanation was sent as a private message: a burst of DMs for a trivial mistake, and nothing at all for members who have DMs closed — their message just vanished without a word. The notice is now posted in the counting channel and deletes itself after 7 seconds: it always arrives, in the right place, and doesn't break the chain of numbers.
  • 🤬 Banned words: per-channel rules — +badword allow #memes <word> tolerates a word in one channel without removing it from the server list, +badword deny #serious <word> bans one extra word just there, and +badword scope only|except #channel… picks where the filter applies. A rule set on a category covers all its channels and their threads. Two safeguards: an only scope with no channel falls back to "everywhere" instead of silently disabling the filter, and an unreadable configuration never disables anything.
  • 🔗 Links: per-channel allow and block lists — +linkwl allow #gif tenor.com allows a domain (and its subdomains) in a given channel, +linkwl deny #serious tenor.com blocks it there even if it is trusted everywhere else, and +linkwl scope only|except #channel… bounds where antilink acts. Enough to finally say "Tenor GIFs only in #gif". The smart analysis — scam, typosquatting, VirusTotal — stays fully active wherever antilink applies: this change narrows the scope and adjusts the lists, it does not touch detection.
  • 🔌 +voicekick — pull someone out of a voice channel — +voicekick @member [reason] (alias +vkick) disconnects a member from their voice channel. It is a warning-level sanction: the person can rejoin, but the action is recorded in their case file and in the log, with the original channel and the reason. Same safeguards as +kick: Move Members permission, hierarchy check, server owner untouchable, and a clear refusal if the member is not in voice (instead of a phantom sanction that disconnected nothing). And like any command, it can be capped: +modlimit add @Moderator voicekick 3/1h lock:6h.
  • ✏️ +editsnipe — the last edited message in the channel — before → after, author, timestamp, with ◀ ▶ navigation through earlier edits. It already existed as a sub-mode (+snipe edit); it now has its own entry point, with exactly the same rendering engine, the same access tier, the same bounded cache (5 edits per channel, 2 h) and the same masking of content removed by security. Bots are never sniped.
  • 📝 +nick and the list of bot-made renames — +nick @member <nickname> changes a member's nickname, +nick @member reset clears it, and above all +nick list shows, page by page, every member the bot has renamed: before → after, when, by whom, and why. Automatic renames are in there too — anti-impersonation, ads in nicknames, shop emoji — which finally answers "why is this member called that?". A rename refused by Discord never enters the list.
  • 🧩 Several embeds in a single message — +embed now accepts --- to separate multiple cards: +embed Part 1 | Text --- Part 2 | More posts two embeds in the same message. Up to 10 (Discord's limit); beyond that it is refused with a clear message rather than a failing send. If the 6,000-character combined budget is exceeded, the extra cards are dropped and reported — since the command message is deleted, the author otherwise assumed everything went through.
  • 📉 Usage caps on moderation commands — the new +modlimit command caps how often a command can be used per role and per time window: +modlimit add @Moderator ban 3/1h. It works for any command (ban, kick, tempsmute, clear, purge, warn, moveall…), and * covers every command at once. When several rules apply to a member, the strictest one decides. The server owner and bot owners are never capped — they are the ones abuse gets reported to.
  • 🚨 Four possible reactions when a cap is exceeded — block (refuse), alert (let it through but warn the owners), both, and lock:2h which suspends the right to that command for the chosen duration. The suspension only affects the targeted command, and a refused attempt is not counted: insisting does not extend the punishment. Alerts are grouped (one per member per command every 5 minutes) so that abuse doesn't drown the signal.
  • 🧮 Counters survive restarts — they are stored, not held in memory: a "3 per hour" cap that reset on every restart would not be a cap, just a suggestion — and restarts mostly happen during incidents, which is exactly when the cap matters. +modlimit status @member shows where someone stands, +modlimit reset clears the counters.
  • 😀 Backups now carry the server's emojis — +backup records the name and image of every emoji (up to 250) and restoring recreates the missing ones, never overwriting one that is already there. Only metadata is stored, not the images: restoring downloads them again. Expect about a minute and a half per fifty emojis — Discord rate-limits this heavily, and slowing it down is better than breaking the channel restore, which is what actually matters after an incident.
  • 💬 Message backup, replayed with the right name and avatar — +backup messages records the last 50 messages per channel (adjustable, 200 max) and +backup messages restore confirm reposts them in the same channels through a webhook, each under its original author's name and avatar. Read before using: these are new messages (no reactions, no threads, no original dates), attachments are not restored (Discord links expire — their count is noted instead), no mention notifies anyone, and reposting runs at one message every 1.2 s to avoid rate limits — the estimated duration is announced before starting. This backup is deliberately kept apart: it never goes into the automatic backup, keeps a single version, stops at 1.5 MB per server, and is wiped with +backup messages purge.
  • 🎯 Backup scope is now a choice — +backup (full: roles + channels + settings + emojis), +backup roles (roles and channels only) and +backup messages (separate, on request). An old trap was closed along the way: backups excluded each other through a hardcoded list of names that had to be kept up to date by hand. That is now automatic — no backup can end up nested inside another, which used to double the database size on every pass.
  • 🎂 Birthdays are announced as a card, with no channel to configure — the announcement moves from plain text to an embed (server colour, member avatar) and, if no channel was chosen, it goes to the general channel instead of never going out at all. +birthday test shows exactly what will be posted — same channel, same card. No announcement can notify @everyone or @here: only the birthday member is mentioned, even if the custom message contains those words.
  • 🎊 Member milestone celebrations — the new +celebration command announces each milestone reached (every 100 members by default, adjustable), with a customisable message ({count}, {step}, {server}). The milestone is recorded before sending: no duplicates, even after a restart. And if several milestones are crossed at once — a wave of joins or catching up after downtime — the milestone is still announced instead of being missed forever.
  • 🚀 A boost card that changes with the number of boosts — when someone boosts the server, the card posted depends on the tier reached: four levels supplied out of the box (first boost, 2, 7 and 14 boosts, Discord's own thresholds), fully replaceable with your own via +celebration boost <threshold> <title> | <description> (variables {user}, {boosts}, {server}, {tier}). Removing a boost announces nothing, of course.
  • 📋 A form when opening a ticket — up to 5 questions defined by the team (+ticket form) are asked before the channel is created, and the answers are posted in it for the staff. Each question can be short or long, optional or required. The form can be switched off without losing the questions; with no question defined, opening stays direct as before. Closing the window without answering costs nothing: no channel is created, and the anti-spam delay is not triggered.
  • 🎨 Ticket button colours are configurable — +ticket settings: blue, grey, green or red for Open a ticket, Close the ticket and Transfer, with an inactive preview of the result. Default colours are unchanged: a server that touches nothing sees no difference.
  • ⏳ +close 24h — close a ticket later — inside a ticket, +close 30m, +close 24h or +close 7j schedules the closure instead of running it: a warning is posted with the deadline and a ✋ Cancel closure button (or +close cancel). The deadline is stored, not merely held in memory: a closure due in 24 h survives the restarts that will happen in between. A malformed duration is rejected rather than treated as a reason — the ticket no longer closes immediately by accident.
  • 🕒 The footer of grouped logs finally says what it means — "counting since restart" described the internal mechanism, not what it changes for the reader. When the bot started during the day, the footer now reads as a full sentence: "⏱️ The bot started today at 14:32 — this total does not cover the whole day." The fact first, the consequence second (whatever happened while the bot was down was never logged, and cannot be recovered afterwards). If the bot has been up since the previous day, the total is complete and the footer stays a plain "Today".
  • 🏷️ Six command shortcuts were overriding each other — four of them made a whole command unreachable — a shortcut won over a command NAME: +stats opened server info instead of game statistics, +defi the weekly objective instead of the daily challenge, +guide the documentation instead of the getting-started guide, +saison the league instead of the games season. Those four commands could only be reached through their secondary shortcuts. Every word now belongs to a single command, and the name always wins. Also: +campagne opens the expedition (it used to start the territory war) and +rang stays on level. Commands that lose a shortcut keep their name and their other aliases (+objectif/+goal/+weekly/+hebdo, +doc/+docs/+wiki, +ligue/+elo/+pvp, +infos).
  • ⚖️ Automatic sanctions no longer start from nothing — with no configuration, automod deleted and warned, but no sanction ever landed: a spammer could simply keep going. You had to know about +punish setup and run it by hand, on every server. Two tiers now apply out of the box: 3 offences in 10 min → 5 min mute, 5 in 30 min → 1 h mute. Deliberately reversible — a setting that applies on its own must never kick or ban on behalf of a server that never asked for it; the harsher ladder is still one word away (+punish setup). An existing configuration is never touched: nothing is written to the database, the default only applies where nothing was set. And "I want no sanctions at all" is finally reachable with +punish off, distinct from "not configured yet" — +punish reset returns to the defaults. +punish list now says which of the three states is active.
  • ⏱️ A closing ticket no longer shows two countdowns — the closing card announced the channel's deletion in two places at once ("deleted in 5s" in the text, plus a "Countdown: 5s" field), both rewritten every second: two timers side by side, which could show different numbers if one rewrite lagged. There is now only one, handed to Discord's own clock — it ticks by itself, without the bot re-editing the message five times for nothing right before deleting the channel. Along the way, the close lock protected +close but not the 🔒 Close ticket button: two quick clicks, or a click during a +close, archived the ticket twice and announced two closures. The lock is now shared by the command, the button and the scheduled deadline.
  • ✋ Claim a ticket — a ✋ Claim button now appears in every ticket, staff only. One click and the ticket is marked as yours, announced in the channel and logged: no more three moderators answering at once, or a ticket everyone assumed someone else had taken. A claimed ticket cannot be taken over by a colleague — the holder must 🔓 release it, or you go through 🔄 Transfer, which formally hands it over. A lead (Manage Channels) can always free a ticket whose holder is away. Switch it off in +ticket settings.
  • 📊 +stafftickets — who actually handles the tickets — each staff member's activity: claims, resolved tickets, and how many they hold right now, with team totals. Paginated. Two details that matter: the resolution credit goes to whoever held the ticket, not to whoever clicked "Close" — a lead wrapping up a colleague's case doesn't take their work; and "in progress" is read from actually-open tickets, not from a subtraction between two counters. A member who left the server stays in the list, marked as such: removing them would skew the totals.
  • 🏆 +claimtop — the claim leaderboard — the podium of staff members who claim the most tickets, with their resolution rate. +claimtop reset clears every counter: administrators only, behind a confirmation button that states exactly what will be erased, and which only the person who ran the command can confirm. It is irreversible — these counters are stored nowhere else.
  • 🗂️ Backups finally cover forums and community servers — a forum channel was backed up… but without its tags, default reaction, sort order, layout, thread slowmode or auto-archive. Restored, it came back stripped of all its organisation — a forum without tags is not a forum. Voice channels likewise lost their bitrate and user limit: the channel existed, but it was no longer the same one. And on a community server, channels came back without the server knowing which were its rules, its updates or its safety alerts — Discord then flagged the setup as incomplete; they are now remembered by name and re-pointed after the restore. Classic channels are backed up exactly as before, not one byte more.
  • ⚡ Restoring now only rebuilds what actually changed — previously, a channel with the right name was treated as "already restored" and left alone. A raid that had wiped its topic, broken its slowmode or opened its permissions to @everyone therefore slipped straight through: the channel was there, but damaged. The restore now compares the real state against the backup and applies only the strict minimum — topic, NSFW, slowmode, category, a voice channel's bitrate and user limit, and above all the permissions, the field that really matters after an attack. What is identical is not touched at all, what is missing is created, what drifted is fixed. It is also far faster: no needless rewriting, and a different ordering of permissions is no longer mistaken for a difference.

    Three modes: smart (default), simple (the old behaviour, only creates what's missing) and purge (smart + deletion of channels absent from the backup). Deletion stays explicit and never implicit: a channel legitimately created after the backup must not vanish unasked — and even in purge, the bot's own channels and a community server's mandatory channels are spared. Along the way a misleading count was fixed: a channel creation refused by Discord was counted as a success, and the bot announced channels that did not exist.
  • ♻️ After a raid, the bot offers to turn back the clock — when an attack is detected then neutralised, it finds the last backup predating the start of the incident and offers to restore it — by DM to the server leads and in the log channel. It picks the most recent one from before the damage, not the oldest: going further back would lose everything legitimately configured in between. With a one-minute margin, because detection only fires after several destructive actions — the attack therefore began slightly before the moment it was spotted, and an automatic backup falling in that window would already contain part of the damage. Nothing is ever restored automatically: two confirmations separate the offer from the action, and only the server leads can give them — letting a detection system, fallible by nature, rewrite an entire server would be disproportionate. If no earlier backup exists, the bot says so plainly instead of offering a later one.
  • 🧩 Before any restore, the bot asks what to do with ITS channels — a backup recreates channels as they were. But some of them aren't yours: stat counters, trophy room, reports channel, games channel, quarantine — the bot created them and keeps them up to date. Asking nothing, a restore recreates the old ones alongside the new and leaves settings pointing at vanished channels. Three buttons now, on every restore (manual as well as post-incident): ✅ Keep (leave them alone), 🗑️ Delete (the backup may already contain its own) or ♻️ Recreate (removed then rebuilt cleanly). The criterion is deliberately narrow: only channels the bot creates itself and knows how to rebuild are involved — your log channel, your welcome or your levels channel are never offered up, they are not his to dispose of.
  • 🎉 +giveaway list — see the running draws — channel, prize, number of winners, entrants, deadline as a countdown and a date, plus a direct link to the message. Every line is numbered, and that number is what you type to cancel: no more hunting for a nineteen-digit message ID.
  • 🚫 A giveaway whose message was deleted can finally be cancelled — until now +giveaway cancel did not exist: the command fell into the creation parser, read "cancel" as a duration and answered "Invalid duration". From the host's point of view: cannot cancel, even with the ID. And if the giveaway's message had been deleted meanwhile, it stayed queued until its deadline, where the bot tried to draw in a ghost channel. Cancelling now exists, and it no longer depends on the message: the draw is removed from the queue first — that is what cancelling means — and updating the display is only attempted afterwards, harmlessly if it fails. Deleted message, deleted channel: the cancellation succeeds either way and says so. You designate the draw by its number, its ID or its link.
  • 🎲 +giveaway reroll [n] — redraw as many winners as you need — +giveaway reroll replaces one winner (the common case: someone unreachable), +giveaway reroll 3 redraws three at once. Winners already drawn are excluded from the pool — including those from an earlier reroll, otherwise the prize could go twice to the same person. An entrant who left the server, lost the required role or got blacklisted in the meantime is skipped, exactly as in the original draw. To make any of this possible, finished draws are now archived with their entrant list (the last twenty): without that, the pool vanished along with the draw and a reroll had nothing to work from.
  • 👻 Arrival ghost ping — when a member joins, the bot mentions them in a channel then deletes the message right away: the notification goes out — the red badge, the phone buzz — but the trace does not. The newcomer opens the server in the right place without one more "@someone" line cluttering an already noisy arrivals channel. Off by default, deliberately: a notification you cannot find afterwards is confusing when you did not ask for it. Set it in +setup → Welcome (picking the channel enables it right away) or with +ghostping #channel. Anti-abuse: one ping per member per hour — someone leaving and rejoining in a loop, or an event replay after a gateway reconnect, cannot turn this into spam. And without permission to delete, the bot sends nothing at all rather than leave a permanent mention.
  • 🎛️ The temporary voice panel gains two buttons — it already offered lock, user limit, rename and kick on every channel creation. Now added: 🔇 Mute everyone — a toggle that unmutes if everyone is already muted, spares the owner (muting yourself then being unable to unmute is the classic trap) and paces the actions so it doesn't get rate-limited halfway — and 👑 Transfer the channel to someone else. The transfer only accepts a member present in the channel (handing it to someone absent means losing it for everyone) and grants the new ownership before removing the old one: should it fail midway, you end up with two owners — awkward but fixable — rather than none.
  • ⏳ +temprole — grant a role for a set time — +temprole add @member @role 7j grants a role that removes itself when it expires. Durations from 1 h to 1 year (12h, 7j, 2s, 1m). +temprole list shows who has what and how long is left, with a countdown that ticks by itself. +temprole with no argument opens a menu: pick the member, the role and the duration from selectors, then confirm — the button stays greyed out until all three are chosen. Security: a role with sensitive permissions is refused… except to an owner, who can already grant it by hand — forbidding it here would protect nothing and would push them to bypass the tool, hence with no deadline and no automatic removal. Two refusals never bend, even for an owner: an integration-managed role, or one above the bot, which Discord would reject anyway. Finally, granting a role the member already had permanently is accepted but flagged — otherwise the role would vanish one day with nobody understanding why.
  • 🔍 +find @member — which voice channel are they in? — the useful answer isn't the channel's name but a clickable link: hunting for a channel in a list of thirty while an incident unfolds is already too slow. The command gives the channel, who is with them, and their state (muted, deafened, screen sharing, camera). It accepts a mention or a raw ID, and when the member is in no voice channel at all, it says so instead of returning an empty card.
  • 🚩 Report a message in two taps — long-press (or right-click) a message → Apps → "Report this message". Nothing to type, no reason to fill in, and the confirmation is visible to you alone: nobody in the channel knows you reported it. Same on a member with "Report this member", which additionally attaches the voice channel they are in and who is present — without that, a voice report arrives after everyone has scattered and can no longer be checked. +report @member is still there, and its reason becomes optional: demanding a few words on one path when two taps suffice on the other would have pushed everyone to the shortest route without ever saying why.
  • 🛡️ A dedicated staff channel for reports — reports used to land in the log channel, mixed in with everything else. They now have #🚩-signalements, created automatically and visible only to staff roles. Those roles are detected automatically: the ticket staff role if one exists, otherwise roles carrying a moderation permission — excluding any held by more than a quarter of the server, because a "Member" role that was mistakenly granted a permission would make the channel public. +report staffrole @role fixes the list by hand. Channel permissions follow roles, never members: a member list desynchronises the moment a moderator changes role, and nobody notices until the day they stop seeing reports. One channel, not one per report: on an active server that would mean dozens of channels per evening.
  • ⚡ Sanction straight from the report, in one click — under every report: Warn · Mute 1 h · Kick · Ban · Delete message · Dismiss. Each button checks on click that whoever pressed it holds the matching permission — a junior moderator sees everything but is cleanly refused what is beyond them — plus the usual hierarchy check. An optional reason is asked for (pre-filled), and once the action is done the card is edited ("handled by…") with its buttons removed: two moderators can't sanction the same case twice. Sanctions go into the case file and remain cappable via +modlimit.

    Banning is restricted to administrators — deliberately stricter than the matching Discord permission: "Ban Members" is often handed to front-line moderators, and banning from a report takes two clicks on the strength of a report nobody may have verified. The button also asks for a confirmation, because it is irreversible. Moderators of course keep their +ban command: it is the shortcut that is restricted, not the power.
  • ✉️ The sanctioned person knows why, and for how long — a mute, kick or ban decided from a report now sends the person a private message: the reason the moderator typed, and the duration ("you can speak again in 1 hour", "you may come back with a new invite", "this ban is permanent"). A sanction without an explanation feels arbitrary, and the first reflex is to open a ticket asking why. One detail that is not a detail: the message goes out BEFORE the sanction is applied — once banned, the member shares no server with the bot and Discord refuses to open the conversation; the explanation would be lost for good, and it is precisely the one explaining the ban. If their DMs are closed, the sanction still applies and staff read "DM not delivered" in the report.
  • 🙈 Anonymous reports, but not untraceable — the staff card says "a member (anonymous)": that is what protects the reporter from retaliation. An administrator can reveal their identity via 👁️ See the reporter — private reply — to spot abusive reporting. Three more safeguards: one report per minute and one per target every 10 minutes; the same message reported by twelve people produces one card reading "12 members reported" instead of twelve drowning the channel; and a staff member gets reported like anyone else — the report lands in the usual staff channel, where the whole team sees it — but they cannot handle the one aimed at them: every button is refused to them, "Dismiss" included. Without that rule, one click would erase the report concerning them and nobody would know it ever existed. Their colleagues handle it normally.
  • 🔢 Stat counters no longer duplicate — deploying counters could create two identical sets of channels. The cause: deployment chains several operations before the first creation (loading members, then deleting the old channels one by one), and two closely-spaced triggers crossed inside that window — both saw the same starting state, then both created. A lock did exist… in one of the six entry points: the assistant and +setup had none, and a command-local lock can't see the other callers anyway. It now lives in the system, shared by deployment and removal. Deployment also became idempotent: a counter already in place is kept as is instead of being destroyed and recreated — ticking one box in the assistant no longer recreates the other five for nothing. And for servers already carrying duplicates: +setstats clean removes them without touching the legitimate counters (an ordinary voice channel is never mistaken for a counter).
  • 🔍 +audit replies in the channel, no longer by DM — the audit detail went out as a private message, with only an acknowledgement in the channel: staff read the report in one place and its confirmation in another, and above all the fix buttons were only usable inside the DM — invisible to the rest of the team, and simply lost when private messages were closed. Everything is now shown where the command was typed, buttons included. The detail (score, at-risk members) therefore becomes visible in the channel: the command stays admin-only, and the message reminds you to run it in a staff-only channel.
  • 📥 Tasks sent to the bot by DM now actually arrive — the owner's inbox was wired correctly… but the permission to receive private messages was missing on the connection side. As a result Discord simply never delivered those messages, with no error and no log line — a perfectly silent failure. Fixed.
  • 🏦 The bank opens: +emprunter when you're broke — a member who lost everything was stuck: no stake possible, so no games, so no way back up. They can now borrow up to 5,000 🟣 (adjustable), with simple interest charged once — the debt never grows on its own while they're away. Three locks against the spiral: no borrowing again while you owe (otherwise it's an infinite aura tap), a cap, and the bank only lends to broke players — it's a safety net, not leverage to bet big on credit. Winnings pay off the debt first, in full, until it's cleared: you play to repay, then you play for yourself. +dette shows where you stand, +dette rembourser tout speeds it up, +dette top lists the most indebted.
  • 💳 A negative balance simply is a debt — playing while broke still sends aura below zero (that's intended), and that negative now counts as a debt to repay: it shows up in +dette and in the debtor ranking. Nothing is converted or copied — the balance stays the balance, and it climbs back on its own with winnings. That's what guarantees a loan and a negative balance can never count the same aura twice: they are two distinct quantities, and a win clears the loan first, then lifts the balance. Example: at −400 with 1,100 borrowed, +dette shows 1,500 to repay, itemised. +dette auto turns off counting the negative as debt.
  • 📊 +ecolog — where the aura actually comes from — the balance said how much someone had, never where from: no way to answer "they earned 400,000 in three days, is that normal?". Every movement is now recorded with its source (farm, wheel, casino, races, heists, battleship, typing race, drops, game bonus, shop, loans). +ecolog @member breaks it down by source over a window of your choice, with gains and losses shown separately — a net of zero can hide "200,000 won then lost again at the casino", which is nothing like inactivity — plus the latest movements. +ecolog serveur (staff) shows which source really feeds the economy, and therefore which one to watch.
  • ⏳ Temporary roles in the shop — +shop addtemprole @VIP 5000 7j puts a role up for rent: it is removed automatically when it expires. A permanently bought role loses its value — six months later half the server wears VIP and it means nothing; a role that expires stays rare and gives a reason to spend again. Durations from 1 h to 1 year (12h, 7j, 2s, 1m). Buying again before expiry extends instead of resetting — otherwise buying at the wrong moment would take away time already paid for. The deadline is stored: a role rented for 30 days survives the restarts that will happen in between, instead of becoming permanent by accident. Same security refusals as permanent roles, re-checked at purchase: never a role with sensitive permissions, above the bot, or integration-managed. Removing a role from sale does not revoke the ones already rented.
  • 🌙 +xpnight — less XP during off-hours — +xpnight 0 6 50 cuts XP gains to 50 % between midnight and 6am (Paris time). Built for voice: someone leaving a channel open all night earned as much as someone taking part during the day. By default only passive gains (messages and voice) are affected — winning a game at 2am is a deliberate action, not presence, and still pays full; +xpnight 0 6 50 tout extends the cut if you want it. The window may cross midnight (23 5 works), and +xpnight with no argument tells you whether the cut applies right now.
  • 🧊 +xpdecay — inactive members' XP can melt — +xpdecay 14 100 7: after 14 days without any activity, a member loses 100 XP every 7 days. Off by default, and enabling it requires a confirmation — it is the only setting in the bot that removes, on its own and repeatedly, something members earned. Safeguards: the floor is level 0 / 0 XP, never negative; a member who writes or speaks once leaves the list immediately; at most 500 members per pass; and absurd settings are refused (a 0 999999 0 would have wiped a server's leaderboard in one go). +xpdecay test simulates the result without removing anything.
  • 📊 +autorank — ranks you earn and can lose — +autorank add 2050 50 @Active: each period, whoever wrote 2,050 messages or spent 50 h in voice gets the role; whoever drops below the threshold loses it and falls to the tier below. The "or" is deliberate: someone who lives in voice and someone who writes a lot are both active. The window is rolling (+autorank periode 7) — an activity rank kept for life isn't an activity rank, just a memory. On the security side, three refusals with no exception: never a role with a sensitive permission (otherwise chatting would become privilege escalation), never a role above the bot, never an integration-managed role — and those checks are re-run on every assignment, not just at setup. The bot only removes roles it granted itself: a role handed out by staff is never undone.
  • 👤 The leaderboard no longer erases those who left — as soon as a member of the top left the server, they turned into "Unknown member" with a grey square instead of an avatar. They did earn their place — erasing them visually is wrong, and it makes the leaderboard look silly ("the server's #2 is a stranger"). The bot now remembers each member's last known name and avatar as they earn XP, and uses it only as a fallback: while the person is still here, Discord is the source of truth. Someone who left now shows with their real name, their real avatar and a (left) marker. Even someone never recorded keeps a stable, distinct label instead of being indistinguishable from every other departed member.
  • 👑 The games champion role no longer vanishes without a word — it changes hands at the end of each season, but the previous champion lost it silently: a golden role, pinned at the top of the member list, evaporating without explanation looks like a punishment. The handover is now announced in the games channel (falling back to the logs). A real gap was closed along the way: the role was only reassigned if somebody had played — on a quiet month, the previous month's champion kept it indefinitely, "champion" of a season they never took part in.
  • 🎖️ A hall of past champions in the trophy room — +sdt now lists the members who have been games champions, with their number of titles, and tells the current crown holder (👑) from past ones (🎖️). Until now, since the role is removed at the end of each season, nobody knew a month later who had worn it: the title is earned, so it stays on display.
  • ⏱️ The trophy room finally catches records as they happen — records were sampled by a poll every 5 minutes: a voice-chat peak lasting three minutes mid-evening was missed entirely, and the "record" on display was just a snapshot of an arbitrary moment. Simultaneous voice and member count records are now measured in real time, the moment someone joins a channel or the server — without a single extra call to Discord, since the information already arrives. The members online record, the only one that genuinely requires querying, goes from 5 to 2 minutes. With three safeguards: a one-second step so a wave of joins can't trigger hundreds of measurements, no two sweeps able to overlap, and no time wasted on servers with no trophy room.
  • ➕ +add and +remove — bring someone into a ticket — inside a ticket, +add @member grants access, +add @role adds every member of that role, and +remove does the opposite. Ticket staff can do everything; the ticket author may invite members to their own case, but not pour a whole role into it — opening a private support channel to dozens of people stays a staff decision. Three safeguards: role members are added one by one and spaced out (a burst gets rate-limited and the bot used to stop halfway without saying so), the command refuses upfront beyond 25 people rather than half-sharing the ticket, and the author can never be removed from their own ticket. The report says exactly who was added, who was already there, and who it failed on.
  • 🚫 Services blacklist — tickets and giveaways — the new +blacklist command stops a member from opening tickets and entering giveaways. It is a dedicated list, deliberately independent from roles: a "banned from tickets" role is bypassed by simply losing it, whereas here the exclusion is tied to the member — it survives a role change, a nickname change, and leaving and coming back. +blacklist add @member [reason], remove, list (paginated) and check @member. The excluded member is told why they were refused: otherwise they open a ticket to ask for the reason… which is exactly what they can no longer do. Three notes: it bans nobody and removes no roles (that's +bl, owner-only, which bans on join), the server owner cannot be added to it, and a member blacklisted after entering a giveaway is excluded from the draw — but can still remove themselves.
  • 🧾 A ticket transcript now contains the form answers — they are posted in a card attached to the welcome message; the archive kept only that message's plain text and wrote [embed] in their place. In other words, the only place the answers were recorded vanished with the channel. Cards are now transcribed in addition to the text, never instead of it.
  • 📟 On-call is on by default — +oncall alerts the team when a critical alert lands while nobody is online. It waited to be enabled server by server, which meant warning nobody exactly where its existence was unknown. It now starts enabled everywhere, including on servers added later. Servers that explicitly turned it off stay off: switching it off is a choice, not the absence of one.
  • 💜 +soutien — reward the members who wear your server tag — Discord lets a member display a server's tag next to their name. The bot sees it and grants the supporter role or roles on its own (several roles supported), then takes them back the moment the tag is no longer displayed. This is verifiable support: Discord decides, not a sentence copied into a status — and the bot asks for no extra permission. Roles are granted the instant the tag changes, and a catch-up sweep goes over the rest for what an event cannot cover (a tag changed while the bot was offline). Same safety rules as everywhere else: never a role with a sensitive permission, never a role the bot cannot manage, never to bots — and a role granted by hand is never taken back, only what the bot itself placed belongs to it.
  • 🔊 A voice channel reserved for supporters — from the +soutien menu, a supporter creates their own voice channel in one click: visible to everyone, but only supporters can enter. It comes with the usual control panel (lock, seat limit, rename, kick) and deletes itself as soon as it empties. Created but never joined, it is removed after 5 minutes — otherwise it would sit there forever.
  • 🚦 Every kind of action can have ITS own log channel — +setlogs routes opens a router: bans to #ban-logs, kicks somewhere else, message purges elsewhere again. Nine precise actions (ban, unban, kick, mute, joins, roles, automatic roles, purges, nicknames) plus the ten broad categories can be routed. An unrouted action falls back to its category, and an unrouted category to the general channel: configuring nothing therefore changes nothing. The bot checks it can actually write in the chosen channel — routing to a channel where it is mute would lose logs silently. And the alert for someone deleting the logs now covers every routed channel, not just the general one.
  • 🧾 A banned member's last messages, kept alongside the ban — banning someone often makes what they wrote disappear (Discord offers to wipe their last 7 days), and the context behind the decision is lost exactly when it will be needed: an appeal, three weeks later. The log now attaches their last messages to the ban entry, captured before anything is deleted. Anything the security layer blocked (phishing, panic word) is never shown again — only the reason is. The log states plainly that it shows what was still in memory, not the full history. Can be turned off.
  • 🤖 Automatic roles no longer drown the role logs — supporter role, level grade, games podium, join role: the bot grants and removes them hundreds of times a day, burying the one change you were looking for — the one made by hand. Three choices in +setlogs routes: leave them mixed in (as before), stop logging them entirely, or give them their own summary, routable to its own channel. The list of affected roles is written nowhere: it is derived from what each module already has configured, so a newly added supporter role is recognised immediately.
  • ⏳ Close a channel "for two hours" and have it reopen by itself — eight new commands: +autolock, +autounlock, +autohide, +autounhide, and the "all channels" variant of each. +autolock #announcements 2h locks now and unlocks at the stated time, with nobody having to remember. The deadline is written to the database, not held by an in-memory timer: a channel closed on Friday evening does not stay closed all weekend because the bot restarted. And at expiry the bot restores the previous state — a channel that was already closed stays closed, instead of being opened by mistake.
  • 🎯 +permsalon — let a role use a command in ONE specific channel — "the host can run +clear in #giveaway, but not in #general". The permission is purely additive: it grants a right, it never removes one, from anyone. A permission set on a category applies to all of its channels. The list of delegable commands is closed and lives in the code — no setting can widen it: excluded are anything touching security or configuration, anything reaching beyond the channel where the command is typed (+purge … all wipes everywhere), and sanctions aimed at a person (a ban does not happen "in a channel").
  • 🙈 Ignore certain channels in channel surveillance — +antiupdate ignore #member-count: counters that rename themselves every ten minutes and temporary voice channels no longer fill the log. Ignoring a category covers all of its channels, including ones created later. Two safeguards: a burst deletion (3 channels in 1 minute) is logged no matter what — that is the signature of a nuke, and the exclusion list must not become the place to hide; and the anti-raid protections have no knowledge of this list, they keep counting and sanctioning every deletion.
  • 🧵 A thread opens by itself under every photo — +autothread #photos: every image posted gets its own thread, so reactions and opinions no longer drown the channel. A message without an image gets none — that is the point. The thread title is configurable ({user}, {sujet}), and tous mode opens a thread under every message if you prefer.
  • 🖼️ Images-only channel — +autothread onlypic on #photos: messages without an image are deleted, with a reminder that erases itself so it does not fill the channel in turn. Members who can manage messages are exempt — without that exception, writing the channel's own rules would be impossible.
  • 🐢 Slowmode in automatically created threads — +autothread lent 30 #photos: threads opened by the bot are born already in slowmode. Set at creation rather than afterwards: between the two there is a window where the thread is open without it, and that is exactly when a burst arrives.
  • 🎨 {MemberBanner} — the member's banner in custom messages — usable in the welcome message, the goodbye message and custom commands, alongside two new neighbours: {MemberPic} (avatar) and {ServerBoostsCount} (boost count). Discord does not ship the banner with the rest: it needs a separate request, which is made only when the message actually asks for it. A member with no banner yields empty text, never "undefined". And the variable list shown by +setwelcome, +setgoodbye and +custom is now derived from the real table — it can no longer advertise a variable that does not exist, nor forget one that was just added.
  • 📦 Surprise crates finally state their rarity — three rarities: 🎁 Normal (87 %, 30-79 XP), ✨ Rare (10 %, 120-199 XP) and 💎 Legendary (3 %, 300-499 XP plus a little aura). They already existed, but the rarity was only named when the crate appeared: on pickup you learned what you won without learning what you had opened. It is now announced at the moment of the claim (emoji, colour and name), including in the private reply to whoever solves a locked crate's riddle. The golden crate is now called Rare; record leaderboards are untouched.
  • 📋 Claimed crates finally go through the log system — their trace bypassed it and wrote straight to the general channel. It therefore ignored per-action routing (+setlogs routes), fed neither the daily digest nor the end-of-day summary, and had no retry on a network hiccup. Fixed, with the rarity, the reward and how the crate was opened (click or riddle) in the embed. Crates get their own routing key: they can go to a channel of their own, and unticking a surveillance category does not switch them off.
  • 📌 Pins become readable — Discord announces "X pinned a message" without saying which one and without linking anywhere. +pinlog on replaces that notice with an embed: who pinned, an excerpt of the message, its author and a direct link. Pins are compared against the ones already known, so a pin made while the bot was offline is found at the next change — and on restart. The very first pass over a channel records what is already there without publishing anything: enabling the module on a channel with forty pins does not publish forty. Destination configurable.
  • 🔒 +lockrole — freeze a role completely — a locked role can no longer be changed: not its name, not its permissions, not its colour, not its separate display, not its mentionability. Any change is undone within the second and logged with its author. This is the answer to the quiet manipulations: renaming "Moderator" to "Member", or stripping a permission from it without anything announcing it. +unlockrole releases the lock. A role sitting above the bot is refused rather than accepting a decorative lock.
  • ↩️ Turning verification off now cleans up — and can be undone — +setverify off only cleared the role: the channel stayed, with a "Verify" button that no longer granted anything, and no way for a newcomer to tell. The now-useless channel is deleted, and owners get an alert with a "That was a mistake — put it all back" button that recreates the channel in its original category, restores the role and reposts the panel. The three ways to disable it (command, +setup, wizard) finally follow the same path.
  • 🌐 +translate — translate a text, or the message you reply to — +translate en Bonjour tout le monde, or reply to a message with +translate es. Fifteen languages, written the way people actually write them (anglais, en, english). The source language is detected automatically. The free service used caps at 500 characters per translation: that is stated plainly when you exceed it, rather than splitting the text into pieces that would contradict each other. Answers cached for ten minutes, one translation every 3 seconds per server, and never a freeze: past 7 seconds without an answer, the bot says so and hands back control.
  • 👁️ +lastseen @member — when a member was last seen ACTIVE — last message, last counted minute of voice, or "in voice right now" when that is the case. This is not a last-connection time: Discord does not report that to anyone, and guessing it would require continuously receiving every member's status on every server. The bot does not ask for that. So the command shows what it based its answer on, and says it outright — "seen three days ago" does not mean "not connected for three days".
  • ⏰ Reminders now understand clock times — and daylight saving — on top of delays (+remind 1h …), you can write +remind 9h30 …, +remind demain 9h … or +remind 25/12 18:00 …. Those times are computed with the offset of the target date, not of the day you type the command: "15 November at 9am" scheduled in midsummer lands at 9am, not 10am. On the spring-forward night, a requested time that does not exist (the clock jumps from 2am to 3am) is flagged and pushed to the first valid instant instead of being silently moved. 9h on its own still means a 9-hour delay, as before.
  • 🗂️ Suggestions can now become FORUM posts — point +setsuggest #ideas at a forum channel and every +suggest creates a full post there: it can be debated in its own thread without polluting the others, votes 👍 / 👎 work exactly as before, and when staff decides, the post gets its tag (✅ Approved / ⛔ Rejected) and is then closed. The mode is inferred from the channel type — no extra setting that could one day disagree with the channel actually configured. Tags already present on your forum ("bug", "idea"…) are kept as they are, IDs included: no already-tagged post loses its tag.
  • 💜 The supporter voice channel now wears the bot's colours — it was called "💜 username", which said nothing about what it was and made it indistinguishable from an ordinary temporary voice channel. It becomes "💜 Soutien · username", in the same signature as the bot's other channels (🏆 trophy room, 🚩 reports): you read what the channel is for first, then whose it is — the useful order when scanning the voice list.
  • ⚖️ +punish setup finally shows the whole strike chain — punishment tiers on one side, thresholds buried in "Advanced settings" on the other, and nothing saying the latter feed the former: setting "5 strikes → ban" without knowing what counts as a strike is configuring half a mechanism. The panel now shows what generates a strike (scam, spam, mass mentions, links, banned words — with each one's real state) and then what strikes trigger. It also warns when no module feeds strikes while tiers are configured: taken separately the two screens looked fine — it is putting them side by side that reveals the gap. Thresholds can be set from either place with a single stored value: the panel's buttons literally call +setup's own code, so the two views cannot drift. +setup points back to +punish in return. (The old +punish setup, which applied the strict ladder at once, is still available via +punish setup strict and as a button in the panel.)
  • 🔔 Alert preferences join the setup assistant — +wizard now has an "Alert preferences" step in the Security group: how the bot flags an alert in the log channel (@everyone, @here, or the alert role, chosen by default). The button opens +alertconfig's own panel — not a copy — including "My personal settings", which stay private.
  • 🔧 +update now updates the CODE, not the packages — the command did not actually touch the code: it ran npm install <package>@latest on every dependency, then restarted.
